  ▐ aura  Done. I read case-10.md and wrote the answer to ANSWER.md.

The case asked what issues Section 3.22 (a customer-relationship MAC representation) presents and how it should be construed and enforced. ANSWER.md contains the analysis only, as instructed:

- **Construction**: it's a relationship-level MAC clause — breached only by an actual, material, durationally significant adverse change with a Material Customer since the Lookback Date; sustained, concentrated revenue decline in the largest accounts (corroborated by the pre-signing "decreasing revenues" deck) qualifies, mere fluctuation doesn't; the two sentences cover different risks (actual deterioration vs. noticed threats), so the buyer proceeds on clause (i) alone absent alleged notice.
- **Enforcement**: the 15-month survival period is a contractual limitations bar; the fraud/intentional-breach carve-out sits in the exclusive-remedy clause and governs remedies, not survival (the *Winshall* approach), so the contract count should be dismissed if untimely — while the fraudulent-inducement count survives if pleaded with particularity, which the internal presentation largely supplies, subject to justifiable reliance and proof at trial.
